\mathbb{W}_{\!\mathtt{b}}
as in Definition
2.3
.
We next denote by
d^{\mathtt{b}}
the graph distance in
\mathbb{W}_{\!\mathtt{b}}
and we denote by

(\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\!\mathtt{b}},\widetilde{d}^{{}_{\,\mathtt{b}}}_{{}^{\!}})
the real tree spanned by
\mathbb{W}_{\!\mathtt{b}}
; with a slight abuse of notation, we suppose that
\mathbb{W}_{\!\mathtt{b}}\!\subset\!\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\!\mathtt{b}}
.
We extend
\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}
as a map from
\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\![0,1]}
to
\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\!\mathtt{b}}
explained in Remark
5.1
and we set

\forall s\!\in\![0,2\#t],\,\forall r\!\in\![0,C_{s}(t)],\quad V^{\mathtt{b}}_{s}(r)=\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(V_{s}(r))\quad\textrm{and}\quad\widehat{V}_{s}^{\mathtt{b}}=V^{\mathtt{b}}_{s}(C_{s}(t))\;,

where we recall that
(V_{s}(\cdot))_{s\in[0,2\#t]}
stands for the spatial contour of
Y
as defined in (
103
).
Note that
\widehat{V}_{s}^{\mathtt{b}}\!=\!\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(\widehat{V}_{s})
. By Remark
5.1
, it turns out that

V^{\mathtt{b}}
is the spatial contour associated with
Y^{\mathtt{b}}
.
Then, denote by

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{b}}
the compact
real tree spanned by
\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}
in
\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\!\mathtt{b}}
.
Namely,

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{b}}\!=\!\{\widehat{V}_{s}^{\mathtt{b}};s\!\in\![0,2\#t]\}\!=\!\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}})\;.

We next extend Lemma
3.7
as follows: since

(\widehat{V}^{\mathtt{b}}_{s})_{s\in[0,2\#t]}
is the continuous interpolation of the path
(Y^{\mathtt{b}}_{v(k)})_{0\leq k\leq 2\#t}
in
\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}
, we easily derive the following from (
33
).

For all
s_{1},s_{2}\!\in\![0,2\#t]
,
there exists a nonnegative r.v.
G_{s_{1},s_{2}}
such that

2G_{s_{1},s_{2}}\!=\!\widetilde{d}\big(\widehat{V}_{s_{1}},\widehat{V}_{s_{2}}\big)-\widetilde{d}^{{}_{\,\mathtt{b}}}\big(\widehat{V}^{\mathtt{b}}_{s_{1}},\widehat{V}^{\mathtt{b}}_{s_{2}}\big)\quad\textit{and}\quad\mathbf{P}\big(G_{s_{1},s_{2}}\!\geq x\big)\leq\mathtt{b}^{2-x},\;x\!\in\![0,\infty)

(120)

This combined with (
119
) shows that
T_{C(t),W}

and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}
are close in a rough sense. It turns out that it is sufficient for the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

5.2
Invariance principle for discrete snakes.

In this section we recall one important result due to Marzouk (Theorem 1
[
29
]
) that is an invariance principle for real valued endpoint processes of discrete snakes. Here we concentrate on the one-dimensional case but we shall need actually a slightly stronger version that holds for path-valued snakes and not only for endpoint processes.

More precisely, let us fix
\gamma\!\in\!(1,2]
and
let
\tau
be a Galton–Watson tree (as in Definition
2.2
) whose offspring distribution
\mu
satisfies
(\mathbf{H})
as in (
1
). Conditionally given
\tau
,
let
(Y_{v})_{v\in\tau}

be a
\mathbb{Z}
-valued branching random walk whose transition kernel
q(y,dy^{\prime})
is that of the simple symmetric random walk on
\mathbb{Z}
.
Then recall from (
105
) the definition of
(\mathcal{W}_{s}(\cdot))_{s\in[0,2\#\tau]}
, the continuous interpolation of the discrete snake (here the tree
T
is
\mathbb{Z}
). Then,

\forall k\!\in\!\{0,\ldots,2\#t\},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{k}=Y_{v(k)}\;,

(121)

where
v(k)\!\in\!\tau
,
0\!\leq\!k\!\leq\!2\#\tau
, stands for
the sequence of vertices of
\tau
visited by the contour (or the depth-first exploration) of
\tau
.
Then, the following result is a special case of Theorem 1 in Marzouk
[
29
]
:

Theorem 5.4

(Theorem 1
[
29
]
)

Let
\gamma
,
\mu
,
\tau
and
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be as in Theorem
4.28
.
Conditionally given
\tau
, let
(Y_{v})_{v\in\tau}
be defined as above
and let
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{k})_{0\leq k\leq 2\#\tau}
be the endpoint process of the snake associated with
(Y_{v})_{v\in\tau}
as in (
121
). Then

\Big(\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}C_{2ns}(\tau),\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\lfloor 2ns\rfloor}\Big)_{\!\!s\in[0,1]}\;\textrm{under $\;\mathbf{P}\big(\cdot\big|\,\#\tau\!=\!n\big)$ }\overset{\textrm{(law)}}{\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}}\;(H_{s},\widehat{W}_{s})_{s\in[0,1]}

where
(H,W)
is the normalised one dimensional Brownian snake with
\gamma
-stable branching mechanism and where
\widehat{W}_{s}\!=\!W_{s}(H_{s})
,
s\!\in\![0,1]
, stands for its endpoint process.

As already mentioned this result actually holds for fairly more general spatial motions in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. It extends earlier results by Janson and Marckert
[
18
]
who considered snakes whose genealogical tree are in the domain of attraction of the Brownian tree. We refer to Marzouk
[
29
]
for more details.
We next derive from Theorem
5.4
the following proposition that holds for
the path-valued continuous interpolation of the discrete snake
(\mathcal{W}_{s}(\cdot))_{s\in[0,2\#\tau]}
.

Proposition 5.5

We keep the same notation and the same assumption as in Theorem
5.4
.
Recall from Definition
4.17

(a)
the metric
\Delta
that makes the space
\mathbf{C}([0,1],\mathbb{R})\!\times\!\mathbf{C}([0,1],\mathbf{C}_{0})
Polish. Then, weakly on that space, the following convergence holds.

\Big(\big(\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}C_{2ns}(\tau))_{\!s\in[0,1]}\,,\big(\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\mathcal{W}_{2ns}(\tfrac{\cdot}{a_{n}})\big)_{\!s\in[0,1]}\Big)\;\textrm{under $\;\mathbf{P}\big(\cdot\big|\,\#\tau\!=\!n\big)$ }\overset{\textrm{(law)}}{\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}}\big(H,W\big).

(122)

By taking the absolute value, this results holds true for the snake reflected at
0
.

Proof.
Let
k_{0}\!=\!0\!\leq\!k_{1}\!\leq\!\ldots\!\leq\!k_{p}\!\leq\!2\#\tau
be integers. Then for all
j\!\in\!\{0,\ldots,p\!-\!1\}
, set
b_{j}\!=\!\min_{k_{j}\leq\ell\leq k_{j+1}}C_{\ell}(\tau)
and
b^{\prime}_{j}\!=\!C_{k_{j+1}}(\tau)\!-\!b_{j}
.
By definition of the branching random walk and its associated snakes, the following holds true conditionally given
\tau
(or
C(\tau)
).

(i)

The paths
S(j)\!:=\!(\mathcal{W}_{k_{j+1}}\big((b_{j}+\ell)\!\wedge\!C_{k_{j+1}}(\tau)\big)\!-\!\mathcal{W}_{k_{j+1}}(b_{j})\big)_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
are (conditionally) independent.

(ii)

The conditional law of
S(j)
is that of a
\mathbb{Z}
-valued symmetric random walk, starting at
0
and stopped at time
b_{j+1}^{\prime}
.

Next observe that
(b,b^{\prime}\!,w,w^{\prime})\!\in\![0,\infty)^{2}\!\times\!\mathbf{C}_{0}^{2}\!\mapsto\!(w(r\wedge b)+w^{\prime}((r\!-\!b)_{+}\!\wedge\!b^{\prime})\!-\!w^{\prime}(0))_{r\in[0,\infty)}
is continuous (recall from Definition
4.17
that
\mathbf{C}_{0}
is equipped with the Polish topology of the convergence on all compact subsets of times, which corresponds for instance to the metric
\delta_{\mathtt{u}}
given in (
82
)). This combined with
(i)
,
(ii)
and easy arguments on linear interpolation imply that for all real numbers
0\!\leq\!s_{1}\!\leq\!\ldots\!\leq\!s_{p}\!\leq\!1
,

(\mathbf{w}^{{}_{(n)}}_{{s_{j}}})_{1\leq j\leq p}
under
\mathbf{P}(\cdot|\#\tau\!=\!n)
converges weakly to
(W_{s_{j}})_{1\leq j\leq p}
on
\mathbf{C}_{0}^{p}
, where we have set

\mathbf{w}^{{}_{(n)}}_{s}(\cdot)\!=\!a_{{}^{n}}^{{}_{-1/2}}\mathcal{W}_{2ns}(\cdot/a_{n})
, for all
s\!\in\![0,1]
.

We next prove the tightness of the rescaled snakes. A standard argument on linear interpolation shows that Theorem
5.4
implies that the continuous endpoint processes
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{{}_{(n)}}
under
\mathbf{P}(\cdot\,|\#\tau\!=\!n)
converge weakly to the endpoint process
\widehat{W}
. This implies that

\forall\varepsilon\!\in\!(0,\infty),\quad\lim_{\eta\rightarrow+}\sup_{n\geq 0}\mathbf{P}(\omega_{\eta}(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{{}_{(n)}})\!\geq\!\varepsilon\,|\#\tau\!=\!n)\!=\!0,

where
\omega_{\eta}(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{{}_{(n)}})
stands for the
\eta
-uniform modulus of continuity of
\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{{}_{(n)}}
over
[0,1]
. We next denote by
\omega_{\eta}(\mathbf{w}^{{}_{(n)}})
the
\eta
-uniform modulus of continuity of
\mathbf{w}^{{}_{(n)}}
with respect to the metric
\delta_{\mathtt{u}}
on
\mathbf{C}_{0}
as introduced in Lemma
4.18
; this lemma asserts that
\omega_{\eta}(\mathbf{w}^{{}_{(n)}})\!\leq\!2\omega_{\eta}(\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{{}_{(n)}})
. This, combined with the weak convergence of the finite dimensional marginals of
\mathbf{w}^{{}_{(n)}}
, entails that the laws of
\mathbf{w}^{{}_{(n)}}
are tight on
\mathbf{C}([0,1],\mathbf{C}_{0})
and we easily get (
122
).
\hfill\blacksquare

5.3
Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Let us fix
\gamma\!\in\!(1,2]
and
let
\tau
be a Galton–Watson tree (as in Definition
2.2
) whose offspring distribution
\mu
satisfies
(\mathbf{H})
as in (
1
).
Then recall from (
98
) the definition of the contour process

(C_{s}(\tau))_{s\in[0,2\#\tau]}
of
\tau
.
Conditionally given
\tau
, let
(Y_{v})_{v\in\tau}

be a
\mathbb{W}_{\![0,1]}
-valued branching random walk with law
Q^{+}_{\boldsymbol{\tau}}

as in Definition
3.1

(ii)
.
Recall from (
103
)
the spatial contour
(V_{s}(\cdot))_{s\in[0,2\#\tau]}
associated with
Y
, and recall from
(
105
) the definition of
(\mathcal{W}_{s}(\cdot))_{s\in[0,2\#\tau]}
, the continuous interpolation of the discrete snake.
We denote by
(\widetilde{\tau},\widetilde{d}^{{\,\tau}}_{{}^{\mathtt{gr}}})
,

(\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\![0,1]},\widetilde{d})
and

(\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\!\mathtt{b}},\widetilde{d}^{\mathtt{b}})

be the real trees spanned by respectively
\tau
,
\mathbb{W}_{\![0,1]}
and
\mathbb{W}_{\!\mathtt{b}}

we assume that
\tau\!\subset\!\widetilde{\tau}
, that
\mathbb{W}_{\![0,1]}\!\subset\!\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\![0,1]}
and that
\mathbb{W}_{\!\mathtt{b}}\!\subset\!\widetilde{\mathbb{W}}_{\!\mathtt{b}}
.
We next couple the free branching random walk
(Y_{v})_{t\in t}
with the
\mathbb{W}_{\mathtt{b}}
-valued one via the
\mathtt{b}
-contraction application
\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}
as in Definition
3.3
: namely we set

Y^{\mathtt{b}}_{v}\!=\!\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(Y_{v})
for all
v\!\in\!\tau
.
By Remark
3.4
,
Y^{\mathtt{b}}
has law
Q^{+\mathtt{b}}_{\mathbf{t}}
as in Definition
3.1

(i)
.
We also set
V^{\mathtt{b}}\!=\!\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(V)
that is the spatial contour of
Y^{\mathtt{b}}
, according to Remark
5.1
. We recall the following notation
\mathcal{R}\!=\!\big\{Y_{v};v\!\in\!\tau\big\}
,
\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}\!=\!\big\{Y^{\mathtt{b}}_{v};v\!\in\!\tau\big\}\!=\!\Phi_{d}(\mathcal{R})
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\!=\!\big\{\widehat{V}_{s};s\!\in\![0,2\#\tau]\big\}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{b}}\!=\!\big\{\widehat{V}^{\mathtt{b}}_{s};s\!\in\![0,2\#\tau]\big\}\!=\!\Phi_{\mathtt{b}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}})
.

We trivially extend
C(\tau)
and
\mathcal{W}
on
[0,\infty)
by taking
C_{s}(\tau)
equal to
0
and by taking
\mathcal{W}_{s}
equal to the null function for all
s\!\in\![2\#\tau,\infty)
.
Let
(a_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be as in Theorem
4.28
.
To simplify notation we set for all
s\!\in\![0,1]
,

\mathbf{h}_{n}(s)\!=\!\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}C_{2ns}(\tau),\quad\mathbf{w}^{(n)}_{s}(r)=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\mathcal{W}_{2ns}\big(\tfrac{r}{a_{n}}\big),\;r\!\in\![0,\infty)\quad\textrm{and}\quad\widehat{\mathbf{w}}^{(n)}_{s}\!=\!\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2ns}.

Observe that
\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\,\#\tau\!=\!n)
-a.s.
(\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)})\!\in\!\boldsymbol{\Sigma}([0,1])
as in Definition
4.17
and we denote by
d_{\mathbf{h}_{n}}
and
d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}}
the corresponding tree and snake pseudo-distances as defined in resp. (
72
) and (
83
). In particular, observe that

\textrm{$\mathbf{P}$-a.s.\penalty\ for all $s_{1},s_{2}\!\in\![0,\infty)$,}\quad\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widetilde{d}\big(\widehat{V}_{2ns_{1}},\widehat{V}_{2ns_{2}}\big)=d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}}(s_{1},s_{2})\;

Proposition
5.5
combined with Lemma
4.20
implies that weakly on
(\mathbf{C}([0,1]^{2},\mathbb{R}),\lVert\cdot\rVert)^{2}

(d_{\mathbf{h}_{n}},d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}})\;\textrm{under $\mathbf{P}\big(\cdot\big|\,\#\tau\!=\!n\big)$ }\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}\;\big(d_{H},d_{H,W}\big),

where
(H,W)
is the normalised one dimensional reflected Brownian snake with
\gamma
-stable branching mechanism as in Definition
4.31
and where
d_{H}
and
d_{H,W}
are the corresponding tree and snake pseudo-distances.

We next set

\forall s_{1},s_{2}\!\in\![0,\infty),\quad\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*}\!(s_{1},s_{2})=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widetilde{d}^{\mathtt{b}}\big(\widehat{V}^{\mathtt{b}}_{2ns_{1}},\widehat{V}^{\mathtt{b}}_{2ns_{2}}\big)\;.

By (
120
), conditionally given
\tau
,
\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*}(s_{1},s_{2})\!\leq\!d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}}(s_{1},s_{2})
and for all
\varepsilon\!\in\!(0,1)
, a.s.

\mathbf{P}\big(d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}}(s_{1},s_{2})\!-\!\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*}(s_{1},s_{2})\!>\!2\varepsilon\,\big|\,\tau\big)\leq\mathtt{b}^{3-\varepsilon\sqrt{a_{n}}}\;,

which implies that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}}(s_{1},s_{2})\!-\!\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*}(s_{1},s_{2})\!>\!2\varepsilon\,\big|\,\#\tau\!=\!n\big)\!=\!0
. Therefore, Proposition
4.4
applies (with
\mathbf{d}_{n}\!=\!d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}}
)
to show that

(\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*},d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}})
under
\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\#\tau\!=\!n)
converge to
(d_{H,W},d_{H,W})
, weakly on
(\mathbf{C}([0,1]^{2},\mathbb{R}),\lVert\cdot\rVert)^{2}
. Actually it is easy to see that

(d_{\mathbf{h}_{n}},\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*},d_{\mathbf{h}_{n},\mathbf{w}^{(n)}})
under
\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\#\tau\!=\!n)
converges to
(d_{H},d_{H,W},d_{H,W})
, weakly on
(\mathbf{C}([0,1]^{2},\mathbb{R}),\lVert\cdot\rVert)^{3}
.

We next recall that
\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\#\tau\!=\!n)
-a.s. the real tree coded by
d_{\mathbf{h}_{n}}
is isometric to

(\widetilde{\tau},\tfrac{{}_{1}}{{}^{a_{n}}}\widetilde{d}^{{}_{\,\tau}}_{{}^{\mathtt{gr}}},\varnothing,\tfrac{{}_{1}}{{}^{n}}\widetilde{\mathtt{m}})
where
\widetilde{\mathtt{m}}\!=\!\delta_{\varnothing}+\mathtt{Length}
. Also recall from (
109
) that
\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\#\tau\!=\!n)
-a.s. the real tree coded by
\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*}
is isometric to
(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{b}},\tfrac{{}_{1}}{{}^{\sqrt{a_{n}}}}\widetilde{d}^{{}_{\,\mathtt{b}}}_{{}^{\!}},\varnothing,\tfrac{{}_{1}}{{}^{2n}}\widetilde{\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{cont}}^{\mathtt{b}})
, where
\widetilde{\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{cont}}^{\mathtt{b}}
is the occupation measure of
(\widehat{V}^{\mathtt{b}}_{s})_{s\in[0,2n]}
, as defined in general in (
107
).
Since
(d_{\mathbf{h}_{n}},\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*})
under
\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\#\tau\!=\!n)
converges to
(d_{H},d_{H,W})
, weakly on
(\mathbf{C}([0,1]^{2},\mathbb{R}),\lVert\cdot\rVert)^{2}
, Proposition
4.14
implies that the pointed measured compact spaces

\big(\widetilde{\tau},\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\widetilde{d}^{{}_{\,\tau}}_{{}^{\mathtt{gr}}},\varnothing,\tfrac{1}{n}\widetilde{\mathtt{m}}\big)
and
\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{b}},\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}\widetilde{d}^{\,\mathtt{b}},\varnothing,\tfrac{1}{2n}\widetilde{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{cont}}\big)
under
\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\#\tau\!=\!n)
jointly converge to resp.
(T_{H},d_{H},r_{H},\mu_{H})
and
(T_{H,W},d_{H,W},r_{H,W},\mu_{H,W})
weakly on
(\mathbb{M},{\boldsymbol{\delta}}_{\mathtt{GHP}})^{2}
, where
T_{H}
and
T_{H,W}
here stand for the real trees coded by resp.
H
and
(H,W)
.

We next recall the following notation.

\mathtt{m}\!=\!\sum_{v\in\tau}\delta_{v},\;\mathbf{m}_{\mathtt{occ}}^{\mathtt{b}}\!=\!\!\sum_{v\in\tau}\delta_{Y^{\mathtt{b}}_{v}},\;\overline{\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{occ}}^{\mathtt{b}}\!=\!\tfrac{1}{n}\mathbf{m}_{\mathtt{occ}}^{\mathtt{b}},\;\mathbf{m}_{\mathtt{count}}^{\mathtt{b}}\!\!=\!\!\sum_{x\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}\!\delta_{x}\;\,\textrm{and}\;\,\overline{\mathbf{m}}_{\mathtt{count}}^{\mathtt{b}}\!\!=\!\frac{{}_{1}}{{}^{\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}}\mathbf{m}_{\mathtt{count}}^{\mathtt{b}}.

By the inequalities specified in Example
4.13
, we get
d_{\mathtt{Haus}}(\tau,\widetilde{\tau})\!\leq\!1
and

d_{\mathtt{Prok}}(\widetilde{\mathtt{m}},\mathtt{m})\!\leq\!3
. Similarly, by
Lemma
5.2
we get
d_{\mathtt{Haus}}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}},\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathtt{b}})\!\leq\!1
and

d_{\mathtt{Prok}}(2\mathbf{m}_{\mathtt{occ}}^{\mathtt{b}},\widetilde{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{cont}})\!\leq\!2
. Thus,

\displaystyle\Big(\big(\tau,\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}d_{{\mathtt{gr}}},\varnothing,\tfrac{1}{n}\mathtt{m}\big)\,,\,\big(\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}},\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{a_{n}}}d_{\mathtt{gr}},\varnothing,\tfrac{1}{n}\mathbf{m}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}}\big)\Big)\quad\textrm{under $\;\mathbf{P}\big(\cdot\big|\,\#\tau\!=\!n\big)$ }

\displaystyle\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{-\!\!\!-\!\!\!-\!\!\!\longrightarrow}\;\big((T_{H},d_{H},r_{H},\mu_{H})\,,\,(T_{H,W},d_{H,W},r_{H,W},\mu_{H,W})\big),

where
d_{\mathtt{gr}}
stands for both graph-tree distances in
\tau
and
\mathbb{W}_{\!\mathtt{b}}
to simplify notation.

We next control
\mathbf{m}_{\mathtt{count}}^{\mathtt{b}}
in terms of
\mathbf{m}_{\mathtt{occ}}^{\mathtt{b}}
thanks to Theorem
1.1
and Lemma
5.3
. To that end, we set
c\!=\!c_{\mu,\mathtt{b}}/2
where
c_{\mu,\mathtt{b}}
is as in Theorem
1.1
; we denote by
u_{0},u_{1},\ldots,u_{\#\tau-1}
the vertices of
\tau
listed in the lexicographical order and we also set

\alpha\!=\!\max_{1\leq i\leq\#\tau}|\#\{Y^{\mathtt{b}}_{u_{j}};0\!\leq\!j\!<\!i\}\!-\!2ci|
and
\beta\!=\!\max_{s\in(0,2\#\tau]}|\#\{\widehat{V}^{\mathtt{b}}_{l}\,;\;0\!\leq\!l\!<\!\!\lceil s\rceil\}\!-\!cs|
.
Theorem
1.1
asserts that for all
\varepsilon\!\in\!(0,\infty)
,
\mathbf{P}(\alpha/n\!>\!\varepsilon\,|\,\#\tau\!=\!n)
tends to
0
as
n\!\rightarrow\!\infty
. By (
111
),
\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\,\#\tau\!=\!n)
-a.s.

\frac{\beta}{n}\!\leq\!\frac{1}{n}(\alpha+3c)+\frac{{ca_{n}}}{n}\max_{s\in[0,1]}h_{n}(s)\;.

Since
(a_{n})
is
\tfrac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}
-regularly varying,
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}a_{n}/n\!=\!0
, which implies that

\mathbf{P}(\beta/n\!>\!\varepsilon\,|\,\#\tau\!=\!n)
tends to
0
as
n\!\rightarrow\!\infty
.

Next, we denote by
d_{\mathtt{Prok}}
the Prokhorov distance on the space of
finite measures on
(\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}},d_{\mathtt{gr}})
and we denote by
d^{(n)}_{\mathtt{Prok}}
the Prokhorov distance on the space of
(\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}},\tfrac{{}_{1}}{{}^{\sqrt{a_{n}}}}d_{\mathtt{gr}})
.
Since
n^{-1}\!\leq\!a_{n}^{{}_{-1/2}}
for all sufficiently large
n
, Remark
4.12
combined with (
112
)
implies that

\mathbf{P}(\,\cdot\,|\,\#\tau\!=\!n)
-a.s.

d^{(n)}_{\mathtt{Prok}}\big(\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}},\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}},\big)\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\overset{\textrm{{Remark \ref{Kremsmunster}}}}{\leq}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\tfrac{{}_{1}}{{}^{\sqrt{a_{n}}}}d_{\mathtt{Prok}}\big(\mathbf{m}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}},\tfrac{n}{\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}\mathbf{m}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}}\big)\!\!\!\!\overset{\textrm{{by (\ref{SktVeitanderGlan})}}}{\leq}\!\!\!\!\tfrac{{}_{1}}{{}^{\sqrt{a_{n}}}}+2q(\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*},\tfrac{4\beta+1}{2nc}),

where
q(\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*},\eta)\!=\!\max\{\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*}(s,s^{\prime});s,s^{\prime}\!\in\![0,1]:|s\!-\!s^{\prime}|\!\leq\!\eta\}
. Since
\mathbf{d}^{*}_{n}\!\rightarrow\!d_{H,W}
weakly on
(\mathbf{C}([0,1]^{2},\mathbb{R}),\lVert\cdot\rVert)
and since for all
\varepsilon\!\in\!(0,\infty)
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{P}(\beta/n\!>\!\varepsilon\,|\,\#\tau\!=\!n)\!=\!0
,
Proposition
4.3
implies that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{P}(q(\mathbf{d}_{n}^{*},\tfrac{4\beta+1}{2nc})\!>\!\varepsilon\,|\,\#\tau\!=\!n)\!=\!0
and thus

\forall\varepsilon\!\in\!(0,1),\quad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(d^{(n)}_{\mathtt{Prok}}\big(\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}},\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}}\big)\!>\!\varepsilon\,\big|\,\#\tau\!=\!n\big)\!=\!0\;.

Then, observe that
\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}\!\leq\!n
and that

\tfrac{\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}{n}\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}}\!=\!\tfrac{1}{n}\mathbf{m}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}}
.
By Remark
4.12
, we get

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{\mathtt{Prok}}\big(c_{\mu,\mathtt{b}}\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}},\tfrac{1}{n}\mathbf{m}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d^{(n)}_{\mathtt{Prok}}\big(c_{\mu,\mathtt{b}}\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}},\tfrac{\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}{n}\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}}\big)+d^{(n)}_{\mathtt{Prok}}\big(\tfrac{\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}{n}\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}},\tfrac{\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}{n}\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big|\tfrac{\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}{n}\!-\!c_{\mu,\mathtt{b}}\Big|+d^{(n)}_{\mathtt{Prok}}\big(\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}},\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}}\big).

Now, recall that
c_{\mu,\mathtt{b}}\!=\!2c
and that
|\tfrac{\#\mathcal{R}_{\mathtt{b}}}{n}\!-\!c_{\mu,\mathtt{b}}|\leq\alpha/n
.
It finally implies that

\forall\varepsilon\!\in\!(0,1),\quad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{P}\big(d^{(n)}_{\mathtt{Prok}}\big(c_{\mu,\mathtt{b}}\overline{\mathbf{m}}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{occ}},\tfrac{1}{n}\mathbf{m}^{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathtt{count}}\big)\!>\!\varepsilon\,\big|\,\#\tau\!=\!n\big)\!=\!0\;,

which completes the proof of Theorem
1.2
.
\hfill\blacksquare
